The company has approximately 1,000 employees and serves over 10,000 healthcare organizations with 55,000+ providers across 40+ medical specialties. It is owned by Vista Equity Partners.\n     9→\n    10→Greenway Health's history is complex, involving multiple acquisitions and rebrands. The lineage traces back to Medical Manager, founded in 1977. Sage Group acquired the healthcare software division from Emdeon for $565 million in 2006 and operated it as Sage Software Healthcare, Inc. In November 2011, Vista Equity Partners acquired it for $320 million and renamed it Vitera Healthcare Solutions. In June 2013, Vitera acquired SuccessEHS, Inc. (a Birmingham-based EHR company). Separately, Greenway Medical Technologies, founded in 1999, had developed an EHR product called PrimeSUITE and went public in February 2012. In late 2013, Vista Equity Partners acquired Greenway Medical Technologies for approximately $644 million ($20.35/share) and combined it with Vitera and SuccessEHS under the new brand \"Greenway Health, LLC.\" This merger brought together three distinct EHR product lines: Intergy (from the Vitera/Sage lineage), PrimeSuite (from Greenway Medical Technologies), and SuccessEHS.\n    11→\n    12→Today Greenway Health markets two primary EHR platforms — Intergy and Prime Suite — along with a suite of shared services including revenue cycle management, patient engagement, analytics, and cloud infrastructure. The company focuses exclusively on ambulatory healthcare practices, from solo providers to large multi-specialty groups, with particular strength in primary care, OB-GYN, orthopedics, pediatrics, FQHCs, and tribal health organizations.\n    13→\n    14→## Product: Intergy EHR\n    15→\n    16→CHPL IDs: 11351 (v21, certified 2023-10-03), 11682 (v22, certified 2025-08-14)\n    17→\n    18→### What It Is\n    19→\n    20→Intergy is a cloud-based (AWS-hosted) integrated Electronic Health Record (EHR) and Practice Management (PM) platform designed for ambulatory healthcare practices of all sizes. It is one of Greenway Health's two flagship EHR products — Intergy comes from the Vitera/Sage/Medical Manager product lineage and is architecturally distinct from PrimeSuite, which came from the original Greenway Medical Technologies acquisition.\n    21→\n    22→The certified module covers the full Intergy platform — both EHR and practice management capabilities are part of the same product. The CHPL metadata lists the SED intended users as \"Healthcare providers in an outpatient ambulatory setting.\"\n    23→\n    24→Both v21 and v22 are certified across a broad range of ONC criteria: clinical data management ((a)(1)-(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14)-(a)(15)), care transitions ((b)(1)-(b)(3)), EHI export ((b)(10)), care plan updates ((b)(9) in v21, (b)(11) in both), patient request for electronic access ((e)(3)), public health reporting ((f)(1)), FHIR API access ((g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10)), and direct messaging ((h)(1)). This is a full-featured ambulatory EHR with integrated practice management.\n    25→\n    26→### Users & Market\n    27→\n    28→Greenway Health serves 10,000+ organizations and 55,000+ providers. Intergy is one of the two main platforms (alongside Prime Suite) supporting this customer base. Specific Intergy customer breakdowns are not publicly disclosed separately.\n    29→\n    30→**Primary users**: Physicians, clinical staff, billing specialists, practice administrators, and front-desk/scheduling staff in ambulatory settings.\n    31→\n    32→**Settings**: Solo practices, small-to-medium group practices, multi-specialty organizations, FQHCs, and tribal health organizations. The product is positioned for ambulatory care broadly rather than a narrow specialty niche.\n    33→\n    34→**Notable mentions**: HealthLinc (FQHC) and New Era Medicine are cited as customers in Greenway testimonials. Greenway won KLAS \"Most Improved Physician Practice Product\" in 2022 for Intergy PM, and was named a top 3 patient portal by KLAS.\n    35→\n    36→**Deployment**: Primarily cloud-based (hosted on AWS), though some sources mention on-premises deployment as an option. Mobile access supported on iPhone, iPad, and Android devices.\n    37→\n    38→### Modules & Functionality\n    39→\n    40→Based on vendor materials, product pages, brochures, and third-party review and forms\n    44→- Specialty-specific template library (configurable to different specialties)\n    45→- Electronic prescribing (e-Rx) including Electronic Prescribing of Controlled Substances continuous assessment\n    90→- Behavioral health guidance, medication compliance support\n    91→- Automated claim creation and eligibility checks for CCM/RPM billing\n    92→\n    93→**Analytics & Reporting:**\n    94→- Intergy Practice Analytics — 5,000 reportable clinical and financial fields\n    95→- Performance dashboards tracking diagnoses, lab results, vitals, visits, risk levels\n    96→- Population health insights\n    97→- Quality measure performance (MIPS reporting support)\n    98→- Regulatory reporting (public health syndromic surveillance, immunization registries)\n    99→\n (bidirectional: orders out, results in)\n   106→- Pharmacy integrations via Surescripts (e-prescribing network)\n   107→- Integration partners include PatientPop, Clearwave, Ambra Health (imaging), CareTrack\n   108→\n   109→### Data & Content\n   110→\n   111→Based on the modules and features described above, Intergy stores and manages a broad range of data:\n   112→\n   113→**Clinical data** (well-documented): Patient demographics, encounter notes/clinical documentation (using 500+ templates), problem lists, medication lists, allergy lists, vital signs, lab orders and results, imaging orders, immunization records, clinical alerts, health reminders/care gap tracking, clinical decision support data, patient health history, and clinical documents (scanned/indexed via Document Manager).\n   114→\n   115→**Prescription data** (well-documented): e-Prescribing records, controlled substance prescriptions (EPCS), PDMP check results, prescription benefit information, medication The extent of stored telehealth session metadata (vs. just charting the encounter) is not detailed.\n   124→\n   125→**Care coordination data** (moderately documented): Chronic care management records, remote patient monitoring data (device readings, health metrics), care coordination notes, health coaching records.\n   126→\n   127→**Analytics/reporting data** (moderately documented): Practice analytics with 5,000 reportable fields spanning clinical and financial data, quality measure calculations, population health metrics.\n   128→\n   129→**Interoperability data** (moderately documented): C-CDA documents sent/received for transitions of care, CommonWell health data exchange records, Direct messages, FHIR API access logs.\n   130→\n   131→**Document management data** (mentioned but not deeply detailed): Scanned documents, faxes, and other digitized records managed through Greenway Document Manager. The types of documents stored (consent forms, referral letters, external records, etc.) are not enumerated but the capability to scan, index, and store arbitrary documents is described.\n   132→\n   133→**What's unclear**: The degree to which certain add-on services (Revenue Services clearinghouse, Clinical Assist AI documentation) store data within the Intergy database vs. in separate systems. The company has approximately 1,000 employees and serves over 10,000 healthcare organizations with 55,000+ providers across 40+ medical specialties. It is owned by Vista Equity Partners.\n\nGreenway Health's history is complex, involving multiple acquisitions and rebrands. The lineage traces back to Medical Manager, founded in 1977. Sage Group acquired the healthcare software division from Emdeon for $565 million in 2006 and operated it as Sage Software Healthcare, Inc. In November 2011, Vista Equity Partners acquired it for $320 million and renamed it Vitera Healthcare Solutions. In June 2013, Vitera acquired SuccessEHS, Inc. (a Birmingham-based EHR company). Separately, Greenway Medical Technologies, founded in 1999, had developed an EHR product called PrimeSUITE and went public in February 2012. In late 2013, Vista Equity Partners acquired Greenway Medical Technologies for approximately $644 million ($20.35/share) and combined it with Vitera and SuccessEHS under the new brand \"Greenway Health, LLC.\" This merger brought together three distinct EHR product lines: Intergy (from the Vitera/Sage lineage), PrimeSuite (from Greenway Medical Technologies), and SuccessEHS.\n\nToday Greenway Health markets two primary EHR platforms — Intergy and Prime Suite — along with a suite of shared services including revenue cycle management, patient engagement, analytics, and cloud infrastructure. The company focuses exclusively on ambulatory healthcare practices, from solo providers to large multi-specialty groups, with particular strength in primary care, OB-GYN, orthopedics, pediatrics, FQHCs, and tribal health organizations.\n\n## Product: Intergy EHR\n\nCHPL IDs: 11351 (v21, certified 2023-10-03), 11682 (v22, certified 2025-08-14)\n\n### What It Is\n\nIntergy is a cloud-based (AWS-hosted) integrated Electronic Health Record (EHR) and Practice Management (PM) platform designed for ambulatory healthcare practices of all sizes. It is one of Greenway Health's two flagship EHR products — Intergy comes from the Vitera/Sage/Medical Manager product lineage and is architecturally distinct from PrimeSuite, which came from the original Greenway Medical Technologies acquisition.\n\nThe certified module covers the full Intergy platform — both EHR and practice management capabilities are part of the same product. The CHPL metadata lists the SED intended users as \"Healthcare providers in an outpatient ambulatory setting.\"\n\nBoth v21 and v22 are certified across a broad range of ONC criteria: clinical data management ((a)(1)-(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14)-(a)(15)), care transitions ((b)(1)-(b)(3)), EHI export ((b)(10)), care plan updates ((b)(9) in v21, (b)(11) in both), patient request for electronic access ((e)(3)), public health reporting ((f)(1)), FHIR API access ((g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10)), and direct messaging ((h)(1)). This is a full-featured ambulatory EHR with integrated practice management.\n\n### Users & Market\n\nGreenway Health serves 10,000+ organizations and 55,000+ providers. Intergy is one of the two main platforms (alongside Prime Suite) supporting this customer base. Specific Intergy customer breakdowns are not publicly disclosed separately.\n\n**Primary users**: Physicians, clinical staff, billing specialists, practice administrators, and front-desk/scheduling staff in ambulatory settings.\n\n**Settings**: Solo practices, small-to-medium group practices, multi-specialty organizations, FQHCs, and tribal health organizations. The product is positioned for ambulatory care broadly rather than a narrow specialty niche.\n\n**Notable mentions**: HealthLinc (FQHC) and New Era Medicine are cited as customers in Greenway testimonials. Greenway won KLAS \"Most Improved Physician Practice Product\" in 2022 for Intergy PM, and was named a top 3 patient portal by KLAS.\n\n**Deployment**: Primarily cloud-based (hosted on AWS), though some sources mention on-premises deployment as an option. Mobile access supported on iPhone, iPad, and Android devices.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on vendor materials, product pages, brochures, and third-party review sites, Intergy includes the following integrated modules and capabilities:\n\n**Clinical / EHR:**\n- Clinical charting and encounter documentation with 500+ customizable templates and forms\n- Specialty-specific template library (configurable to different specialties)\n- Electronic prescribing (e-Rx) including Electronic Prescribing of Controlled Substances (EPCS)\n- In-workflow Prescription Drug Monitoring Program (PDMP) checking\n- Real-time prescription benefit information\n- Health reminders — upcoming, due, and overdue care gaps displayed per patient\n- Clinical alerts\n- Lab ordering and results integration (Greenway Lab Interface integrates with Intergy for ordering lab tests and receiving results)\n- Imaging ordering\n- Clinical decision support\n- Greenway Clinical Assist — AI-powered clinical documentation tool\n- Greenway Document Manager — digital document workflows (scanning, indexing, storing documents)\n- Patient photo ID and insurance card imaging\n- USPS Web Tools integration for accurate patient address recording\n- CommonWell Health Alliance integration for health data exchange across organizations\n\n**Practice Management / Administrative:**\n- Patient scheduling and appointment management across multiple locations\n- Rules-based scheduling to optimize patient attendance\n- Patient check-in workflows\n- Resource management across sites\n- Insurance eligibility verification at check-in (automated eligibility verification)\n- Multi-location support with centralized scheduling\n\n**Billing / Revenue Cycle:**\n- Comprehensive billing and claims management\n- Claim scrubbing (checking insurance claims before submission)\n- Charge posting\n- Accounts receivable tracking\n- Financial reporting\n- Integration with Greenway Revenue Services (clearinghouse services) — clearinghouse for claim submission, payment posting, and denial management\n- Medical coding support\n- Greenway Health Pay (integrated payment processing)\n\n**Patient Engagement:**\n- Greenway Patient Portal — patients can view medical records, lab results, medications; send secure messages to providers; schedule appointments; request prescription refills; pay bills online; access health history forms. Portal is SMART-on-FHIR powered and VDT (View-Download-Transmit) compliant. Supports health advocate access (one account for multiple practices).\n- Greenway Patient Connect — online appointment scheduling and patient self-service\n- Multi-channel customizable patient reminders and notifications\n- Patient messaging (appointment reminders, procedure instructions)\n- Secure messaging between patients and providers\n\n**Telehealth:**\n- Greenway Telehealth — HIPAA-compliant, device-agnostic telehealth integrated with the EHR. Providers can launch telehealth visits and manage visit workflows directly within Intergy.\n\n**Care Coordination:**\n- Chronic Care Management (CCM) — integrated solution with dedicated health coaching teams to manage patients with chronic conditions\n- Remote Patient Monitoring (RPM) — real-time health data capture and transmission from monitoring devices, enabling continuous assessment\n- Behavioral health guidance, medication compliance support\n- Automated claim creation and eligibility checks for CCM/RPM billing\n\n**Analytics & Reporting:**\n- Intergy Practice Analytics — 5,000 reportable clinical and financial fields\n- Performance dashboards tracking diagnoses, lab results, vitals, visits, risk levels\n- Population health insights\n- Quality measure performance (MIPS reporting support)\n- Regulatory reporting (public health syndromic surveillance, immunization registries)\n\n**Interoperability & Integrations:**\n- FHIR API (g)(10) certified — standardized API access for third-party apps\n- CommonWell Health Alliance membership for cross-network data exchange\n- Direct messaging ((h)(1)) for secure provider-to-provider communication\n- C-CDA document exchange for transitions of care\n- Lab interface integrations (bidirectional: orders out, results in)\n- Pharmacy integrations via Surescripts (e-prescribing network)\n- Integration partners include PatientPop, Clearwave, Ambra Health (imaging), CareTrack\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the modules and features described above, Intergy stores and manages a broad range of data:\n\n**Clinical data** (well-documented): Patient demographics, encounter notes/clinical documentation (using 500+ templates), problem lists, medication lists, allergy lists, vital signs, lab orders and results, imaging orders, immunization records, clinical alerts, health reminders/care gap tracking, clinical decision support data, patient health history, and clinical documents (scanned/indexed via Document Manager).\n\n**Prescription data** (well-documented): e-Prescribing records, controlled substance prescriptions (EPCS), PDMP check results, prescription benefit information, medication The EHI Export package contains multiple sets of files to support both the Single Patient and Patient Population Export.  </p>\n    31→\t\t\t<p>The minimum supported version for EHI export is: Intergy version 21.24.00.00</p>\n    32→\t\t\t <div class=\"header-buttons\">\n    33→\t\t\t\t<a class=\"button-primary\" href=\"./EHI/Viewer/index.html\">Data Tables</a>\n    34→\t\t\t</div></br>\n    35→\t\t\n    36→\t\t<h2>Single Patient Export</h2>\n    37→\t\t\n    38→\t\t<p>The following files are provided as part of the Intergy's Single Patient EHI Export package:\n    39→\t\t<ul>\n    40→\t\t<li><p><b>Documents</b>. The Documents folder contains Patient Clinical Documents, Images, and other Clinical Summary documents stored in the chart.\n    41→\t\tIn this folder, the user will see files, such as GenFile in JPEG format and TMSCatalog in ZIP format. \n    42→\t\tThe General Images are stored as GenFile with a unique GenFileSID and the Patient Clinical Documents are stored as TMSCatalog with a unique CatalogSID in the database.</p>\n    43→\n    44→\t\t<p><i><b>Note:</b> The Documents are exported as ZIP files and contain an embedded RTF/PDF/DOC file. In some cases, the embedded document can be a ZIP file that can still be opened in Adobe Viewer or Word or WordPad, and other editors. In this case, rename the file with the .RTF extension before opening in the editor.</i></li></p>\n    45→\n    46→\t\t<li><p><b>Images</b>. The Images folder includes the patient images (like X-rays) along with their annotations, imaged supporting documents (like advance directives), imaged diagnostic imaging/procedure reports, impressions, and more in TIF and Video Clip format. The annotations are the separate XML files with an extension of .ann where the user's/provider's annotations are exported as a base64 encoded string.</p>\n    47→\t\t<p>Some images within the index do not bear a file extension.  For these files, a user or program may drop the file directly into a document processing program (such as Microsoft Word, Google Docs, etc.) and the image will open. It can then be viewed, stored, or converted to a new format.</p></li>\n    48→\n    49→\t\t<li><p><b>Tables</b>. The Tables folder includes information about each database table that was exported. The user can see the table details with table fields in the CSV format. For information about the data included in each table, refer to the <a href=\"./EHI/Viewer/index.html\">Data Tables.</a></p>\n    50→\t\t<p><i><b>Note</b>: In the table, the description \"FK (Foreign Key)\" means that the field is referencing details from the Parent table where it was originated in the database. The default value \"?\" means that the value defined for that table field in the database is null or empty.</i></li></p>\n    51→\t\t</ul>\n    52→\n    53→\t\t</p>\n    54→\n    55→\t\t<h2>Patient Population Export</h2>\n    56→\t\t<p>The following files are provided as part of the Intergy's Patient Population EHI Export package:\n    57→\t\t<ul>\n    58→\t\t<li><p><b>Documents</b>. The Documents folder contains all the documents in the system including blob data (raw data) in ZIP format. The user will see folders, such as <b>GenFileBlob</b>, <b>TMSCatalogBlob</b>, and <b>PracPersonSecureMsgBlob</b>. Each folder has an upper limit of 10,000 documents. If the limit is exceeded,  the documents are exported into multiple folders depending on the number of documents available in the system.</p>\n    59→\t\t<p>For <b>GenFileBlob</b>, files may be exported as several types of extension. If the file fails to open when selected, the file should be renamed from the current file extension (.jpg) to .zip. Files can then be extracted from the zipped folder as their intended extension, which can be for any file type (for example: .cfp, .cfm, .sch, .rdf, .doc, .txt). Files that require unzipping are a type that is not included in the single-patient EHI export as they may contain several patients' data (ex: HCFA-1500 forms, report templates, etc.). The data in these files is available in other tables within the export. For this reason, users may wish to ignore these files when interpreting their export.</p>\n    60→\t\t\n    61→\t\t<p>Regarding <b>TMSCatalogBlob</b>, although the export contains a TMSCatalogBlob table, as documented in the Data Dictionary, it is not a table listing for the blob tables. Instead, the <b>TMSCatalog.csv</b> file serves as an index of the <b>\"Documents\\TMSCatalogBlob\"</b> folder contents. Each record in TMSCatalog.csv includes a CatalogSID that maps directly to a ZIP file in the TMSCatalogBlob folder. For example, a record with CatalogSID = 27 in the CSV corresponds to the _27.zip file in the folder.</p>\n    62→\t\t<img src=\"./assets/tmscatalogblob.jpg\" alt=\"tmscatalogblob example\">\n    63→\t\t</li>\n    64→\n    65→\t\t<li><p><b>Images</b>. All Images in the system are exported in TAR Format. Each tar file has an upper limit of 5GB. The naming convention of each tar file depends on the storage device from which the image has been exported. The ImageIndexFile.csv file in the folder contains the path of the images. To view an image, unzip the respective TAR file. Refer to the path in the Image Index File and find the required image. The TAR file can be unzipped using an archiving application, such as 7Zip, WinRAR, or gzip.</p>\n    66→\t\t<p>Some images within the index do not bear a file extension.  For these files, a user or program may drop the file directly into a document processing program (such as Microsoft Word, Google Docs, etc.) and the image will open. It can then be viewed, stored, or converted to a new format.</p></li>\n    67→\n    68→\t\t<li><p><b>Tables</b>. The Tables folder includes all the tables containing EHI that have been exported in the CSV format. This data may be viewed in Microsoft Excel or other spreadsheet applications. For information about the data included in each table, refer to the <a href=\"./EHI/Viewer/index.html\">Data Tables.</a></p></li>\n    69→\t\t\n    70→\t\t<li><p><b>Bulk Export Status</b>. This file in CSV format shows the status of each table, document, and image exported. This status file consists of Table Name and Success/Failure indicating if a table/document/image export was completed successfully or with an error.</p></li>\n    71→\t\t</ul>\n    72→\t\t</p>\n    73→\t<h2>Intergy EHI Export Notes</h2>\n    74→\t<p>The MEDCIN Findings for both Single Patient and Patient Population export are indicated as:</p>\n    75→<p><strong>Pregnancy Findings:</strong> When the <strong>PatientProblem</strong> table is exported, there could be a <strong>PatientProblem</strong> record indicating that the patient is pregnant. The EHI Export package contains multiple sets of files to support both the Single Patient and Patient Population Export.  </p>\n\t\t\t<p>The minimum supported version for EHI export is: Intergy version 21.24.00.00</p>\n\t\t\t <div class=\"header-buttons\">\n\t\t\t\t<a class=\"button-primary\" href=\"./EHI/Viewer/index.html\">Data Tables</a>\n\t\t\t</div></br>\n\t\t\n\t\t<h2>Single Patient Export</h2>\n\t\t\n\t\t<p>The following files are provided as part of the Intergy's Single Patient EHI Export package:\n\t\t<ul>\n\t\t<li><p><b>Documents</b>. The Documents folder contains Patient Clinical Documents, Images, and other Clinical Summary documents stored in the chart.\n\t\tIn this folder, the user will see files, such as GenFile in JPEG format and TMSCatalog in ZIP format. \n\t\tThe General Images are stored as GenFile with a unique GenFileSID and the Patient Clinical Documents are stored as TMSCatalog with a unique CatalogSID in the database.</p>\n\n\t\t<p><i><b>Note:</b> The Documents are exported as ZIP files and contain an embedded RTF/PDF/DOC file. In some cases, the embedded document can be a ZIP file that can still be opened in Adobe Viewer or Word or WordPad, and other editors. In this case, rename the file with the .RTF extension before opening in the editor.</i></li></p>\n\n\t\t<li><p><b>Images</b>. The Images folder includes the patient images (like X-rays) along with their annotations, imaged supporting documents (like advance directives), imaged diagnostic imaging/procedure reports, impressions, and more in TIF and Video Clip format. The annotations are the separate XML files with an extension of .ann where the user's/provider's annotations are exported as a base64 encoded string.</p>\n\t\t<p>Some images within the index do not bear a file extension.  For these files, a user or program may drop the file directly into a document processing program (such as Microsoft Word, Google Docs, etc.) and the image will open. It can then be viewed, stored, or converted to a new format.</p></li>\n\n\t\t<li><p><b>Tables</b>. The Tables folder includes information about each database table that was exported. The user can see the table details with table fields in the CSV format. For information about the data included in each table, refer to the <a href=\"./EHI/Viewer/index.html\">Data Tables.</a></p>\n\t\t<p><i><b>Note</b>: In the table, the description \"FK (Foreign Key)\" means that the field is referencing details from the Parent table where it was originated in the database. The default value \"?\" means that the value defined for that table field in the database is null or empty.</i></li></p>\n\t\t</ul>\n\n\t\t</p>\n\n\t\t<h2>Patient Population Export</h2>\n\t\t<p>The following files are provided as part of the Intergy's Patient Population EHI Export package:\n\t\t<ul>\n\t\t<li><p><b>Documents</b>. To view an image, unzip the respective TAR file. Refer to the path in the Image Index File and find the required image. The TAR file can be unzipped using an archiving application, such as 7Zip, WinRAR, or gzip.</p>\n\t\t<p>Some images within the index do not bear a file extension.  For these files, a user or program may drop the file directly into a document processing program (such as Microsoft Word, Google Docs, etc.) and the image will open. *\n * Usage: bun run extract-tables.ts\n *\n * Input:  ../viewer/Contracts/*.htm  (261 table HTML files)\n *         ../viewer/Contracts/include/DBDescriptions.js (table descriptions)\n * Output: ./tables.json             (full structured data dictionary)\n *         ./tables-summary.json     (compact summary with table/field counts)\n *         ./coverage-report.json    (accounting of parse results)\n */\n\nimport { readdir, readFile, writeFile } from \"fs/promises\";\nimport { join, basename } from \"path\";\n\nconst CONTRACTS_DIR = join(import.meta.dir, \"..\", \"viewer\", \"Contracts\");\nconst OUTPUT_DIR = import.meta.dir;\n\ninterface Field {\n  name: string;\n  datatype: string;\n  default_value: string | null;\n  null_option: string; // MANDATORY | OPTIONAL\n  comment: string;\n}\n\ninterface Relationship {\n  table: string;\n  join_phrase: string;\n  on_delete: string;\n}\n\ninterface TableDef {\n  name: string;\n  description: string;\n  last_updated: string | null;\n  intergy_version: string | null;\n  fields: Field[];\n  parent_tables: Relationship[];\n  child_tables: Relationship[];\n}\n\ninterface ParseResult {\n  success: boolean;\n  table?: TableDef;\n  error?: string;\n  file: string;\n}\n\nfunction extractText(html: string): string {\n  return html\n    .replace(/<br\\s*\\/?>/gi, \" \")\n    .replace(/<[^>]*>/g, \" \")\n    .replace(/&nbsp;/g, \" \")\n    .replace(/&amp;/g, \"&\")\n    .replace(/&lt;/g, \"<\")\n    .replace(/&gt;/g, \">\")\n    .replace(/&quot;/g, '\"')\n    .replace(/&#39;/g, \"'\")\n    .replace(/\\s+/g, \" \")\n    .trim();\n}\n\nfunction parseTablePage(html: string, filename: string): TableDef {\n  const tableName = basename(filename, \".htm\");\n\n  // Extract metadata\n  const lastUpdatedMatch = html.match(/Last Updated:\\s*([^<]+)/);\n  const versionMatch = html.match(/Intergy Version:\\s*([^<]+)/);\n  const descMatch = html.match(/<h3>\\s*Description:\\s*(.*?)<\\/h3>/is);\n\n  const lastUpdated = lastUpdatedMatch ? lastUpdatedMatch[1].trim() : null;\n  const intergyVersion = versionMatch ? versionMatch[1].trim() : null;\n  const description = descMatch ? extractText(descMatch[1]) : \"\";\n\n  // Extract fields from table definition\n  const fields: Field[] = [];\n\n  // Find the main table definition section (first table after \"Table Definition\")\n  const tableDefSection = html.split(/<a name=\"parentrel\">/i)[0] || html;\n\n  // Match rows in the table definition\n  const rowRegex =\n    /<tr>\\s*<td[^>]*>([^<]*)<\\/td>\\s*<td[^>]*>([^<]*)<\\/td>\\s*<td[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/td>\\s*<td[^>]*>([^<]*)<\\/td>\\s*<td[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/td>\\s*<\\/tr>/gi;\n  let match: RegExpExecArray | null;\n  while ((match = rowRegex.exec(tableDefSection)) !== null) {\n    const fieldName = extractText(match[1]);\n    const datatype = extractText(match[2]);\n    const rawDefault = extractText(match[3]);\n    const nullOption = extractText(match[4]);\n    const comment = extractText(match[5]);\n\n    if (fieldName && fieldName !== \"Field\") {\n      // Skip header row\n      fields.push({\n        name: fieldName,\n        datatype: datatype,\n        default_value:\n          rawDefault === \"?\" || rawDefault === \"\" ? null : rawDefault,\n        null_option: nullOption,\n        comment: comment,\n      });\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Extract parent tables\n  const parentTables: Relationship[] = [];\n  const parentSection = html.match(\n    /<a name=\"parentrel\"><\\/a>[\\s\\S]*?<table[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/table>/i\n  );\n  if (parentSection) {\n    const relRowRegex =\n      /<tr>\\s*<td>\\s*<a[^>]*>([^<]*)<\\/a>\\s*<\\/td>\\s*<td>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/td>\\s*<td>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/td>\\s*<\\/tr>/gi;\n    while ((match = relRowRegex.exec(parentSection[1])) !== null) {\n      parentTables.push({\n        table: extractText(match[1]),\n        join_phrase: extractText(match[2]),\n        on_delete: extractText(match[3]),\n      });\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Extract child tables\n  const childTables: Relationship[] = [];\n  const childSection = html.match(\n    /<a name=\"childrel\"><\\/a>[\\s\\S]*?<table[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/table>/i\n  );\n  if (childSection) {\n    const relRowRegex =\n      /<tr>\\s*<td>\\s*<a[^>]*>([^<]*)<\\/a>\\s*<\\/td>\\s*<td>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/td>\\s*<td>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/td>\\s*<\\/tr>/gi;\n    while ((match = relRowRegex.exec(childSection[1])) !== null) {\n      childTables.push({\n        table: extractText(match[1]),\n        join_phrase: extractText(match[2]),\n        on_delete: extractText(match[3]),\n      });\n    }\n  }\n\n  return {\n    name: tableName,\n    description,\n    last_updated: lastUpdated,\n    intergy_version: intergyVersion,\n    fields,\n    parent_tables: parentTables,\n    child_tables: childTables,\n  };\n}\n\n// Also parse descriptions from DBDescriptions.js\nasync function loadDescriptions(): Promise<Map<string, string>> {\n  const descMap = new Map<string, string>();\n  try {\n    const jsContent = await readFile(\n      join(CONTRACTS_DIR, \"include\", \"DBDescriptions.js\"),\n      \"utf-8\"\n    );\n    const descRegex = /new Table\\('([^']+)'\\s*,\\s*\"([^\"]*)\"\\)/g;\n    let m: RegExpExecArray | null;\n    while ((m = descRegex.exec(jsContent)) !== null) {\n      descMap.set(m[1], m[2]);\n    }\n  } catch {\n    console.warn(\"Could not load DBDescriptions.js\");\n  }\n  return descMap;\n}\n\nasync function main() {\n  const files = (await readdir(CONTRACTS_DIR)).filter(\n    (f) =>\n      f.endsWith(\".htm\") && f !== \"DBTOC.htm\" && !f.startsWith(\"include\")\n  );\n\n  console.log(`Found ${files.length} table files to parse`);\n\n  const descriptions = await loadDescriptions();\n  const results: ParseResult[] = [];\n  const tables: TableDef[] = [];\n\n  for (const file of files.sort()) {\n    try {\n      const html = await readFile(join(CONTRACTS_DIR, file), \"utf-8\");\n      const table = parseTablePage(html, file);\n\n      // Supplement description from DBDescriptions.js if empty\n      if (!table.description && descriptions.has(table.name)) {\n        table.description = descriptions.get(table.name)!;\n      }\n\n      tables.push(table);\n      results.push({ success: true, table, file });\n    } catch (err: any) {\n      results.push({ success: false, error: err.message, file });\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Write full data dictionary\n  await writeFile(\n    join(OUTPUT_DIR, \"tables.json\"),\n    JSON.stringify(tables, null, 2)\n  );\n\n  // Write compact summary\n  const summary = tables.map((t) => ({\n    name: t.name,\n    description: t.description,\n    field_count: t.fields.length,\n    parent_count: t.parent_tables.length,\n    child_count: t.child_tables.length,\n    fields: t.fields.map((f) => f.name),\n  }));\n  await writeFile(\n    join(OUTPUT_DIR, \"tables-summary.json\"),\n    JSON.stringify(summary, null, 2)\n  );\n\n  // Write coverage report\n  const successful = results.filter((r) => r.success);\n  const failed = results.filter((r) => !r.success);\n  const zeroFields = successful.filter(\n    (r) => r.table && r.table.fields.length === 0\n  );\n\n  const coverageReport = {\n    total_files_discovered: files.length,\n    total_files_parsed: successful.length,\n    parse_failures: failed.map((r) => ({\n      file: r.file,\n      error: r.error,\n    })),\n    zero_field_tables: zeroFields.map((r) => r.file),\n    total_tables: tables.length,\n    total_fields: tables.reduce((sum, t) => sum + t.fields.length, 0),\n    total_relationships: tables.reduce(\n      (sum, t) => sum + t.parent_tables.length + t.child_tables.length,\n      0\n    ),\n    field_count_distribution: {\n      \"0_fields\": tables.filter((t) => t.fields.length === 0).length,\n      \"1-10_fields\": tables.filter(\n        (t) => t.fields.length >= 1 && t.fields.length <= 10\n      ).length,\n      \"11-30_fields\": tables.filter(\n        (t) => t.fields.length >= 11 && t.fields.length <= 30\n      ).length,\n      \"31-50_fields\": tables.filter(\n        (t) => t.fields.length >= 31 && t.fields.length <= 50\n      ).length,\n      \"51+_fields\": tables.filter((t) => t.fields.length > 50).length,\n    },\n  };\n  await writeFile(\n    join(OUTPUT_DIR, \"coverage-report.json\"),\n    JSON.stringify(coverageReport, null, 2)\n  );\n\n  console.log(`\\nResults:`);\n  console.log(`  Parsed: ${successful.length}/${files.length} files`);\n  console.log(`  Failed: ${failed.length}`);\n  console.log(\n    `  Total fields: ${tables.reduce((s, t) => s + t.fields.length, 0)}`\n  );\n  console.log(\n    `  Total relationships: ${tables.reduce((s, t) => s + t.parent_tables.length + t.child_tables.length, 0)}`\n  );\n  if (zeroFields.length > 0) {\n    console.log(\n      `  Tables with 0 fields: ${zeroFields.length} (${zeroFields.map((r) => r.file).join(\", \")})`\n    );\n  }\n  if (failed.length > 0) {\n    console.log(`  Failures:`);\n    failed.forEach((r) => console.log(`    ${r.file}: ${r.error}`));\n  }\n}\n\nmain().catch(console.error);\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":137,"cache_read_input_tokens":67148,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":137},"output_tokens":18,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e6f8b572-a3fe-4971-a647-3622fe18a2c8","uuid":"58d0ce56-5f5e-448c-8bcc-4479720b0443"}

Links to Greenway Health Developer Platform (FHIR API) — this is separate from the EHI export.\n\n**Step 7 — Check for additional downloadable files:**\nNo PDFs, ZIPs, CSVs, or other downloadable files were linked from any of the pages. The entire data dictionary is HTML-based.\n\n**Step 8 — Screenshots taken in browser:**\n- Main EHI export page (full page screenshot)\n- Data Tables viewer with left-pane table list and right-pane table definition\n\n## What Was Found\n\nGreenway Health provides a well-structured, purpose-built EHI export documentation site for Intergy. This is a genuine (b)(10) implementation — not a repackaged FHIR API.\n\n### Export Format\nThe Intergy EHI export produces a package containing three types of content:\n\n1. **Tables** — CSV files, one per database table. The export covers 261 tables across the full Intergy database schema. Each CSV can be opened in Excel or other spreadsheet applications.\n\n2. **Documents** — Clinical documents, patient clinical summaries, and other chart documents exported as ZIP files containing embedded RTF/PDF/DOC files. Two types:\n   - `GenFile` (general files/images, keyed by GenFileSID)\n   - `TMSCatalog` (transcription management system documents, keyed by CatalogSID)\n   - `PracPersonSecureMsg` (secure messages, population export only)\n\n3. **Images** — Patient images (X-rays, advance directives, diagnostic imaging/procedure reports) in TIF and video clip format, with XML annotation files (.ann, base64 encoded). In population export, images are packaged in TAR files (up to 5GB each) with an `ImageIndexFile.csv` for path lookup.\n\n### Two Export Modes\n- **Single Patient Export**: Documents, Images, and Tables folders for one patient.\n- **Patient Population Export**: All patients in the organization. Documents are organized into subfolders (GenFileBlob, TMSCatalogBlob, PracPersonSecureMsgBlob) with a 10,000 document per folder limit. Images in TAR archives with index files. Includes a Bulk Export Status CSV showing success/failure per table/document/image.\n\n### Data Dictionary\nThe data dictionary is the crown jewel of this documentation. For each of the 261 tables:\n- **Table description** explaining its purpose\n- **Field definitions** with: field name, SQL data type, default value, null option (MANDATORY/OPTIONAL), and a human-readable comment\n- **Parent table relationships** with join conditions and delete cascade behavior\n- **Child table relationships** with join conditions and delete cascade behavior\n- **Version metadata**: Last Updated 9/24/2025, Intergy Version 22.00.00.00\n\nTotal: 261 tables, 4,529 fields, 1,052 documented relationships.\n\n### MEDCIN Cross-Reference\nThe main page provides specific MEDCIN finding ID mappings:\n- Pregnancy findings: MEDCIN 30596 → SNOMED 77386006 / ICD-10 Z33.1\n- Smoking status: MEDCIN 100000511 with 8 ClinicalLookupCode values mapped to SNOMED codes\n\n### Minimum Version\nEHI export requires Intergy version 21.24.00.00 or later.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThis export is remarkably comprehensive. fields\n\n**Not explicitly present but likely not EHI:**\n- Audit logs (correctly excluded — operational security data)\n- System configuration tables (correctly excluded — infrastructure)\n- Template research mentions integrated telehealth (Greenway Telehealth), but no telehealth-specific tables appear. Telehealth encounters may be recorded as regular Encounters with a visit type flag rather than separate tables.\n- Chronic Care Management (CCM) and Remote Patient Monitoring (RPM) — mentioned in product research as integrated capabilities. May be captured through existing encounter/order tables or through the ExtAttributeCode/EAObjectData extensibility framework. No dedicated CCM/RPM tables are visible.\n- Practice Analytics data — product has 5,000 reportable fields, but analytics/reporting data is aggregate/operational, not per-patient EHI.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses a **database dump approach** — CSV files corresponding to database tables, plus native document/image files. This is not FHIR, C-CDA, or any healthcare interoperability standard. It is, however, arguably the most honest and complete approach to (b)(10) compliance:\n\n- **CSV tables** preserve the full relational database structure with all fields, including internal IDs that enable cross-table joins\n- **Documents** are exported in their native formats (RTF, PDF, DOC) inside ZIP containers\n- **Images** are exported in native TIF/video formats with XML annotations\n- **Relationships** between tables are fully documented with join conditions\n\nThe format is appropriate for the data. A relational database export with documented schema is the most faithful way to export \"all electronic health information\" from a relational database system. A FHIR translation would inevitably lose data that doesn't map to FHIR resources.\n\nA third party could reconstruct the patient record from this export given the data dictionary — the join conditions, foreign key annotations, and table descriptions provide the necessary context to navigate the relational model.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- Every one of 261 tables has a dedicated documentation page\n- Field-level documentation includes data types, nullability, and descriptive comments\n- Foreign key relationships are explicitly documented with join conditions and cascade behaviors\n- The data dictionary is searchable via the viewer interface\n- Version and date stamps are present on every page (Last Updated: 9/24/2025, Intergy Version: 22.00.00.00)\n- The main page provides clear guidance on export structure, including notes about file format quirks (e.g., ZIP files that need renaming to .RTF)\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- Some table descriptions are empty (\"No Description Available\" or blank) — about 20-30 tables\n- Many FK fields simply say \"FK\" in the comment without specifying which lookup table/type they reference (though parent table relationships section helps)\n- No sample export files or worked examples are provided\n- No explicit documentation of which tables are exported in Single Patient vs. Population mode\n- Value sets for coded fields are often described indirectly (\"defined in LookupCode table for lookup type 'X'\") rather than enumerated — the LookupCode table itself is in the export, so values are available but require cross-referencing\n- The MEDCIN finding cross-reference is helpful but limited to pregnancy and smoking status — other MEDCIN IDs in EncounterFinding are not mapped\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n**Field-level granularity**: Excellent. Every field has a name, SQL data type (INTEGER, CHARACTER(n), DECIMAL(n,m), DATE, etc.), default value, mandatory/optional flag, and a descriptive comment.\n\n**Relationships**: Thoroughly documented. Parent and child table relationships include join conditions and delete cascade behavior (RESTRICT, CASCADE, SET NULL). This is essential for navigating the relational model.\n\n**Value sets**: Partially documented. Coded fields reference lookup types in the LookupCode table but don't enumerate the actual codes inline. Some clinical codes (MEDCIN, SNOMED, ICD-10) are cross-referenced on the main page. The ClinicalLookupCode table provides mappings for clinical coded values.\n\n**Versioning**: Each table page shows the Intergy version (22.00.00.00) and last update date (9/24/2025). No change history or diff between versions is provided.\n\n### Overall Assessment\n\nGreenway Health has done genuine (b)(10) work here. This is not a FHIR API repackaged as EHI export documentation. The export is a faithful database dump covering 261 tables across every major data domain in the product — demographics, clinical encounters, problems, medications, allergies, labs, immunizations, vitals, care plans, billing, claims, insurance, specialty clinical data (OB/GYN, cardiology), documents, images, referrals, workers' comp, advance directives, and more.\n\nThe documentation is one of the more thorough EHI export data dictionaries we've encountered. The combination of field-level definitions, relationship documentation, and export format descriptions provides enough information for a technically competent consumer to understand and process the export. The 4,529 documented fields across 261 tables with 1,052 explicit relationships represents substantial investment in documentation.\n\nThe main gaps are cosmetic rather than substantive: some tables lack descriptions, some FK comments are terse, and there are no sample files. But the core data dictionary is solid, and the export approach (CSV database dump + native documents/images) is well-suited to the (b)(10) requirement of exporting all electronic health information.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://ehi.greenwayhealth.com/Intergy/IntergyEHIExport.html\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (all content accessible via curl; JavaScript loads table list in viewer but underlying data is in static .htm files)\n- Navigation complexity: one_click (main page → Data Tables viewer → individual table pages)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\nNone. The documentation was fully accessible, well-structured, and easy to navigate. Each is a structured HTML page with field definitions, data types, default values, null options, comments, parent tables, and child tables.\n\n**Step 6 — Check parent home page:**\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://ehi.greenwayhealth.com/default.htm\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0'\n```\nParent page is Greenway's EHI export hub covering both Intergy and Prime Suite. Links to each product's EHI export page. Also mentions Greenway Insights, Patient Portal, and Electronic Case Reporting are covered by the respective EHR product's export. Links to Greenway Health Developer Platform (FHIR API) — this is separate from the EHI export.\n\n**Step 7 — Check for additional downloadable files:**\nNo PDFs, ZIPs, CSVs, or other downloadable files were linked from any of the pages. The entire data dictionary is HTML-based.\n\n**Step 8 — Screenshots taken in browser:**\n- Main EHI export page (full page screenshot)\n- Data Tables viewer with left-pane table list and right-pane table definition\n\n## What Was Found\n\nGreenway Health provides a well-structured, purpose-built EHI export documentation site for Intergy. This is a genuine (b)(10) implementation — not a repackaged FHIR API.\n\n### Export Format\nThe Intergy EHI export produces a package containing three types of content:\n\n1. **Tables** — CSV files, one per database table. The export covers 261 tables across the full Intergy database schema. Each CSV can be opened in Excel or other spreadsheet applications.\n\n2. **Documents** — Clinical documents, patient clinical summaries, and other chart documents exported as ZIP files containing embedded RTF/PDF/DOC files. Two types:\n   - `GenFile` (general files/images, keyed by GenFileSID)\n   - `TMSCatalog` (transcription management system documents, keyed by CatalogSID)\n   - `PracPersonSecureMsg` (secure messages, population export only)\n\n3. **Images** — Patient images (X-rays, advance directives, diagnostic imaging/procedure reports) in TIF and video clip format, with XML annotation files (.ann, base64 encoded). In population export, images are packaged in TAR files (up to 5GB each) with an `ImageIndexFile.csv` for path lookup.\n\n### Two Export Modes\n- **Single Patient Export**: Documents, Images, and Tables folders for one patient.\n- **Patient Population Export**: All patients in the organization. Documents are organized into subfolders (GenFileBlob, TMSCatalogBlob, PracPersonSecureMsgBlob) with a 10,000 document per folder limit. Images in TAR archives with index files. Includes a Bulk Export Status CSV showing success/failure per table/document/image.\n\n### Data Dictionary\nThe data dictionary is the crown jewel of this documentation. For each of the 261 tables:\n- **Table description** explaining its purpose\n- **Field definitions** with: field name, SQL data type, default value, null option (MANDATORY/OPTIONAL), and a human-readable comment\n- **Parent table relationships** with join conditions and delete cascade behavior\n- **Child table relationships** with join conditions and delete cascade behavior\n- **Version metadata**: Last Updated 9/24/2025, Intergy Version 22.00.00.00\n\nTotal: 261 tables, 4,529 fields, 1,052 documented relationships.\n\n### MEDCIN Cross-Reference\nThe main page provides specific MEDCIN finding ID mappings:\n- Pregnancy findings: MEDCIN 30596 → SNOMED 77386006 / ICD-10 Z33.1\n- Smoking status: MEDCIN 100000511 with 8 ClinicalLookupCode values mapped to SNOMED codes\n\n### Minimum Version\nEHI export requires Intergy version 21.24.00.00 or later.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThis export is remarkably comprehensive. The 261 tables span virtually every data domain that Intergy stores about patients:\n\n**Clearly covered (with deep table structures):**\n- **Demographics/Person** (34 tables, 415 fields): Person, Entity, Address, Phone, Email, PersonRace, PersonTribalAffiliation, PersonRelationship, PersonHistory, DOBHistory, SexHistory, PersonName, Employee, Occupation — including full change history tables\n- **Clinical encounters** (15 tables, 344 fields): Encounter, EncounterFinding, EncounterFindingHistory, EncounterDiagnosis, EncounterEducation, EncounterEvent, EncounterVital, EncounterVitalSet, ClinicalCase, ClinicalCustomFinding\n- **Problems/Diagnoses** (8 tables, 148 fields): PatientProblem, PatientProblemHistory, PatientProblemCodeLink, Diagnosis, Ailment with diagnosis/note/procedure sub-tables\n- **Medications/Prescriptions** (18 tables, 318 fields): PatientRx, PatientRxActivity, PatientRxDenial, PatientRxFill, RxChangeRequest, RxDiagnosis, RxEligibility (PBM eligibility/benefits), DURAlert, RxNote, RxRequest\n- **Allergies** (5 tables across demographics): PersonAllergy, PersonAllergyHistory, PersonAllergyReaction, PersonAllergyReactionHist, Allergy\n- **Lab orders/results** (17 tables, 341 fields): Full lab workflow from LabOrder through LabOrderTest, LabOrderTestResult, LabOrderTestResultNote, LabOrderTestSpecimen, with lab/provider/specimen metadata\n- **Immunizations** (5 tables, 120 fields): PatientVacDose, PatientVacDoseAction, PatientVacDoseReaction, Vaccine definitions, VaccineReaction definitions\n- **Vitals** (3 tables): EncounterVital, EncounterVitalSet, VitalType\n- **Care plans** (12 tables, 153 fields): PatientCarePlan, goals, interventions, outcomes, health concerns — with full history tables\n- **Documents/Imaging** (13 tables, 218 fields): Document, DocumentInterpretation, DocumentNote, GenFile/GenFileBlob, TMSCatalog/TMSCatalogBlob, RISStudy/RISVisit (radiology), CLWCorrespondence (clinical letter writer)\n- **Billing/Claims** (43 tables, 855 fields): Account, Charge, ChargeActivity, ChargeCoverage, Claim, ClaimCharge, Payment, PaymentAssignment, PlanClaim with full remittance detail (PlanClaimChargeRemit, PlanClaimChargeRemitAdj, PlanClaimChargeRemitRmk), Adjustment, CopayActivity, AccountBill, AccountStatement\n- **Insurance/Coverage** (11 tables, 218 fields): Carrier, Plan, PlanGroup, Policy, PolicyMember, Eligibility/EligibilityBenefit/EligibilityComment, PriorAuthInfo\n- **OB/GYN specialty** (12 tables, 284 fields): OBPregnancy, OBInitialVisit, OBPrenatalVisit, OBPostpartumVisit, OBBirth, OBUltraSound — full obstetrics workflow\n- **Cardiology** (4 tables, 54 fields): CardioOrder, CardioComponent, CardioOrderComponent\n- **Orders** (8 tables, 115 fields): PatientOrderSet, PatientOMOrderLineItem with AUC (Appropriate Use Criteria) tracking\n- **Referrals** (11 tables, 136 fields): Referral, ReferralDiagnosis, ReferralTreatmentPlan/Result, RefDoctor\n- **Workers' Comp** (3 tables, 52 fields): WorkersComp, WorkersCompDiagnosis, WorkersCompProcedure\n- **PHI/Consent** (5 tables, 67 fields): PHIAuthorization, PHIConsent, PHIDisclosure with activity tracking\n- **Appointments** (5 tables, 76 fields): Appointment, ApptQuestionnaire, ApptRecall, ApptRoom, ApptStaff\n- **Secure messaging** (2 tables): PracPersonSecureMessage, PracPersonSecureMsgBlob\n- **Implantable devices** (2 tables): PatImplantableDevice, PatImplantableDeviceActivity\n- **Advance directives** (2 tables): AdvanceDirective, AdvanceDirectiveActivity\n- **Lookup/reference codes** (4 tables): LookupCode, AltLookupListCode, ExtAttributeCode, ClinicalLookupCode\n- **Extended attributes** (1 table): EAObjectData — extensible data model for custom fields\n\n**Not explicitly present but likely not EHI:**\n- Audit logs (correctly excluded — operational security data)\n- System configuration tables (correctly excluded — infrastructure)\n- Template definitions (correctly excluded — system configuration)\n- Print/fax logs (correctly excluded — operational)\n\n**Potentially missing but borderline:**\n- Telehealth session metadata — the product research mentions integrated telehealth (Greenway Telehealth), but no telehealth-specific tables appear. Telehealth encounters may be recorded as regular Encounters with a visit type flag rather than separate tables.\n- Chronic Care Management (CCM) and Remote Patient Monitoring (RPM) — mentioned in product research as integrated capabilities. May be captured through existing encounter/order tables or through the ExtAttributeCode/EAObjectData extensibility framework. No dedicated CCM/RPM tables are visible.\n- Practice Analytics data — product has 5,000 reportable fields, but analytics/reporting data is aggregate/operational, not per-patient EHI.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses a **database dump approach** — CSV files corresponding to database tables, plus native document/image files. This is not FHIR, C-CDA, or any healthcare interoperability standard. It is, however, arguably the most honest and complete approach to (b)(10) compliance:\n\n- **CSV tables** preserve the full relational database structure with all fields, including internal IDs that enable cross-table joins\n- **Documents** are exported in their native formats (RTF, PDF, DOC) inside ZIP containers\n- **Images** are exported in native TIF/video formats with XML annotations\n- **Relationships** between tables are fully documented with join conditions\n\nThe format is appropriate for the data. A relational database export with documented schema is the most faithful way to export \"all electronic health information\" from a relational database system. A FHIR translation would inevitably lose data that doesn't map to FHIR resources.\n\nA third party could reconstruct the patient record from this export given the data dictionary — the join conditions, foreign key annotations, and table descriptions provide the necessary context to navigate the relational model.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- Every one of 261 tables has a dedicated documentation page\n- Field-level documentation includes data types, nullability, and descriptive comments\n- Foreign key relationships are explicitly documented with join conditions and cascade behaviors\n- The data dictionary is searchable via the viewer interface\n- Version and date stamps are present on every page (Last Updated: 9/24/2025, Intergy Version: 22.00.00.00)\n- The main page provides clear guidance on export structure, including notes about file format quirks (e.g., ZIP files that need renaming to .RTF)\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- Some table descriptions are empty (\"No Description Available\" or blank) — about 20-30 tables\n- Many FK fields simply say \"FK\" in the comment without specifying which lookup table/type they reference (though parent table relationships section helps)\n- No sample export files or worked examples are provided\n- No explicit documentation of which tables are exported in Single Patient vs. Population mode\n- Value sets for coded fields are often described indirectly (\"defined in LookupCode table for lookup type 'X'\") rather than enumerated — the LookupCode table itself is in the export, so values are available but require cross-referencing\n- The MEDCIN finding cross-reference is helpful but limited to pregnancy and smoking status — other MEDCIN IDs in EncounterFinding are not mapped\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n**Field-level granularity**: Excellent. Every field has a name, SQL data type (INTEGER, CHARACTER(n), DECIMAL(n,m), DATE, etc.), default value, mandatory/optional flag, and a descriptive comment.\n\n**Relationships**: Thoroughly documented. Parent and child table relationships include join conditions and delete cascade behavior (RESTRICT, CASCADE, SET NULL). This is essential for navigating the relational model.\n\n**Value sets**: Partially documented. Coded fields reference lookup types in the LookupCode table but don't enumerate the actual codes inline. Some clinical codes (MEDCIN, SNOMED, ICD-10) are cross-referenced on the main page. The ClinicalLookupCode table provides mappings for clinical coded values.\n\n**Versioning**: Each table page shows the Intergy version (22.00.00.00) and last update date (9/24/2025). No change history or diff between versions is provided.\n\n### Overall Assessment\n\nGreenway Health has done genuine (b)(10) work here. This is not a FHIR API repackaged as EHI export documentation. The export is a faithful database dump covering 261 tables across every major data domain in the product — demographics, clinical encounters, problems, medications, allergies, labs, immunizations, vitals, care plans, billing, claims, insurance, specialty clinical data (OB/GYN, cardiology), documents, images, referrals, workers' comp, advance directives, and more.\n\nThe documentation is one of the more thorough EHI export data dictionaries we've encountered. The combination of field-level definitions, relationship documentation, and export format descriptions provides enough information for a technically competent consumer to understand and process the export. The 4,529 documented fields across 261 tables with 1,052 explicit relationships represents substantial investment in documentation.\n\nThe main gaps are cosmetic rather than substantive: some tables lack descriptions, some FK comments are terse, and there are no sample files. But the core data dictionary is solid, and the export approach (CSV database dump + native documents/images) is well-suited to the (b)(10) requirement of exporting all electronic health information.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://ehi.greenwayhealth.com/Intergy/IntergyEHIExport.html\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (all content accessible via curl; JavaScript loads table list in viewer but underlying data is in static .htm files)\n- Navigation complexity: one_click (main page → Data Tables viewer → individual table pages)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\nNone.
